\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Phi

Figure 2.
Controlled sequent calculus for a default theory
\langle\mathcal{W},\mathcal{D}\rangle

Definition 3.2
.

Let
\langle\mathcal{W},\mathcal{D}\rangle
be any default logic. The controlled sequent calculus
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
for
\langle\mathcal{W},\mathcal{D}\rangle
can be defined by adopting the rules in Figure
2
, provided that the conditions
(i)-(vii)
below are fulfilled.

(i)

For each instance of
ax
, one of the following conditions holds:
\Theta=\Lambda=\{A\}
for some literal
A
,
\Theta=\{A,\neg A\}
or
\Lambda=\{A,\neg A\}
for some atom
A
,
\Theta\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\Lambda
belongs to
\mathsf{top_{cp}^{\#}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}W)
, with
W
being the conjunction of the formulas in
\mathcal{W}
.

(ii)

For each instance of binary structural and logical rules,
{\bf T}={\bf T_{1}}\cup{\bf T_{2}}
and
{\bf S}={\bf S_{1}}\cup{\bf S_{2}}
.

(iii)

For each instance of
cut_{asa}
,
A
is an atom occurring in some formula in
\Gamma\cup\mathcal{W}
.

(iv)

For each instance of
\sigma
,
{\bf S}\subseteq{\bf S^{\prime}}
.

(v)

For any default rule of the form
\dfrac{B:C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}}{D}
in
\mathcal{D}
, if
\mathsf{top_{r}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}B)=\{\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\Theta_{1},\ldots,\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\Theta_{m}\}
with
m>0
and
\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\Phi
belongs to
\mathsf{top_{r}^{\#}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}D)
, then there exists an extra-logical rule of the form:

\{\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.48051pt\hskip-3.85413pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.85413pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.85413pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{i}}$}}\hskip-3.85413pt\hskip 3.85413pt}$}\hskip-3.85413pt\hskip-4.48051pt\raisebox{6.86388pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{i}}$}}\hskip-4.48051pt\hskip 4.48051pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}

\delta

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Phi

with
{\bf T}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}{\bf T_{i}}\cup\{\{(\bigwedge_{i=1}^{m}(\bigvee\Theta_{i}))^{{\sf f}}\}\}
and
{\bf S}=\bigcup\limits_{i=1}^{m}{\bf S_{i}}\cup\{\{\neg C_{1}^{{\sf f}},\ldots,\neg C_{k}^{{\sf f}}\}\}
.

If
\mathsf{top_{r}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}B)=\varnothing
and
\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\Phi
belongs to
\mathsf{top_{r}^{\#}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}D)
, then there exists an extra-logical rule of the form:

\delta

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Phi

with
{\bf T}
being
\{\{\top^{\sf f}\}\}
and
{\bf S}
being
\{\{\neg C_{1}^{\sf f},\ldots,\neg C_{k}^{\sf f}\}\}
.

(vi)

For any extra-logical rule

\{\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.48051pt\hskip-3.85413pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.85413pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.85413pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{i}}$}}\hskip-3.85413pt\hskip 3.85413pt}$}\hskip-3.85413pt\hskip-4.48051pt\raisebox{6.86388pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{i}}$}}\hskip-4.48051pt\hskip 4.48051pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}

\delta

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Phi

with
m\geq 0
, if
\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\Phi^{\prime}
occurs in
\mathsf{top_{r}^{\#}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}(\bigvee\Phi)\wedge W)
without belonging to
\mathsf{top_{r}^{\#}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}W)
, then there is an extra-logical rule of the form:

\{\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.48051pt\hskip-3.85413pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.85413pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.85413pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{i}}$}}\hskip-3.85413pt\hskip 3.85413pt}$}\hskip-3.85413pt\hskip-4.48051pt\raisebox{6.86388pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{i}}$}}\hskip-4.48051pt\hskip 4.48051pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}

\delta

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Phi^{\prime}

(vii)

For any pair of extra-logical rules

\{\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.4927pt\hskip-3.86632pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.86632pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.86632pt\raisebox{-6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{i}^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-3.86632pt\hskip 3.86632pt}$}\hskip-3.86632pt\hskip-4.4927pt\raisebox{7.85669pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{i}^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-4.4927pt\hskip 4.4927pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}

\delta

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Phi

\{\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.4927pt\hskip-3.86632pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.86632pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.86632pt\raisebox{-6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{i}^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-3.86632pt\hskip 3.86632pt}$}\hskip-3.86632pt\hskip-4.4927pt\raisebox{7.85669pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{i}^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-4.4927pt\hskip 4.4927pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta_{i}\}_{m+1\leq i\leq n}

\delta

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Phi^{\prime}

with
m+n\geq 0
, if
\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\Psi
occurs in
\mathsf{top_{r}^{\#}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}(\bigvee\Phi)\wedge(\bigvee\Phi^{\prime}))
without belonging to
\mathsf{top_{r}^{\#}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}(\bigvee\Phi))
,
\mathsf{top_{r}^{\#}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}(\bigvee\Phi^{\prime}))
or
\mathsf{top_{r}^{\#}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}W)
, then there exists an extra-logical rule of the form:

\{\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.4927pt\hskip-3.86632pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.86632pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.86632pt\raisebox{-6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{i}^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-3.86632pt\hskip 3.86632pt}$}\hskip-3.86632pt\hskip-4.4927pt\raisebox{7.85669pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{i}^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-4.4927pt\hskip 4.4927pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq n}

\delta

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Psi

with
{\bf S}={\bf S_{1}}\cup{\bf S_{2}}
and
{\bf T}={\bf T_{1}}\cup{\bf T_{2}}
.

Condition
(i)
ensures that the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculus incorporates the extra-logical axioms from
\mathcal{W}
, while condition
(v)
guarantees the presence of extra-logical rules corresponding to the default rules in
\mathcal{D}
. In turn, closure conditions
(vi)
and
(vii)
are required to ensure that the conclusions of extra-logical rules are closed under Contraction and Cut (cf. Lemmas
3.3
and
3.8
, and Theorems
3.1
,
3.4
,
3.5
,
3.6
).

It is easy to find cases in which there exists a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivation of a controlled sequent
\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
even though
\bigvee\Delta
does not belong to any modified extension of
\langle\mathcal{W}\cup\Gamma,\mathcal{D}\rangle
.

Example 3.1
.

Let
\langle\mathcal{W},\mathcal{D}\rangle
be defined as follows:
\mathcal{W}=\varnothing
and
\mathcal{D}=\Big\{\dfrac{\top:p}{p}\Big\}
. Consider the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivation:

\delta

\neg p\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p

with
{\bf T}=\{\{\top^{\sf f}\}\}
and
{\bf S}=\{\{\neg p^{\sf f}\}\}
. It is easy to check that
p
does not belong to any modified extension of
\langle\mathcal{W}\cup\{\neg p\},\mathcal{D}\rangle
.

As a result, we need to offer a criterion to single out
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivations which deliver
m
-credulous consequences of
\mathcal{W}\cup\Gamma
from those which do not. To this aim, we make some additions to our terminological and conceptual apparatus.

For any extra-logical rule
\delta
in
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
, we define the label
\delta_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}
as follows:

(a)

if
\delta
is generated in accordance with point
(iii)
in Definition
3.2
, then
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\Big\{\dfrac{B:C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}}{D}\Big\}
;

(b)

if
\delta
is generated from extra-logical rules
\delta^{\prime}
and
\delta^{\prime\prime}
with labels
\delta_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}
and
\delta_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}
, respectively, in accordance with points
(iv)-(v)
in Definition
3.2
, then
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}=\mathcal{D}_{1}\cup\mathcal{D}_{2}
.

For each
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivation
\pi
we say that a default rule
\dfrac{B:C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}}{D}

belongs to
def(\pi)
if and only if there is (at least) one extra-logical rule labelled
\delta_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}
which is applied in
\pi
and such that
\dfrac{B:C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}}{D}
belongs to
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
. Moreover, we employ
def^{\prime}(\pi)
to denote
def(\pi_{1})\cup\cdots\cup def(\pi_{m})
whenever
\pi_{1},\ldots,\pi_{m}
are the immediate subderivations yielding the premises of the lowermost extra-logical rules applications in
\pi
.

We shall use
D_{\pi}
to refer to the conjunction of the formulas in
concl(def(\pi))
, and
E_{\pi}
to denote the conjunction of the formulas in
concl(def^{\prime}(\pi))
.

Definition 3.3
.

Let
\pi
be a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivation of
\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
. The sequent
\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
is

(i)

sound under conditions if and only if
([W,E_{\rho}]\cup\Gamma)\,||\,\langle{\bf V},\varnothing\rangle
for any subderivation
\rho
of
\pi
with
\Pi\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.4472pt\hskip-3.4472pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.4472pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.4472pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf U}$}}\hskip-3.4472pt\hskip 3.4472pt}$}\hskip-3.4472pt\hskip-3.3875pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf V}$}}\hskip-3.3875pt\hskip 3.4472pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma
as conclusion;

(ii)

sound under constraints if and only if
([W,D_{\pi}]\cup\Gamma)\,||\,\langle\varnothing,{\bf S}\rangle
;

(iii)

sound if and only if it is sound under conditions and sound under constraints.

Definition 3.4
.

Let
\pi
be a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivation of
\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
. Then,
\pi
is a proof of
\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
if and only if any sequent
\Pi\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.4927pt\hskip-3.86632pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.86632pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.86632pt\raisebox{-6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-3.86632pt\hskip 3.86632pt}$}\hskip-3.86632pt\hskip-4.4927pt\raisebox{6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-4.4927pt\hskip 4.4927pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma
occurring in
\pi
is sound – and a paraproof, otherwise.

Example 3.2
.

Let
\mathcal{W}=\varnothing
and
\mathcal{D}=\Big\{\dfrac{p:q}{r}\,;\,\dfrac{q:s}{t}\Big\}
. The following derivation
\pi
in the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculus for
\langle\mathcal{W},\mathcal{D}\rangle
is a paraproof:

ax

p\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p

\delta_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}

p\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}r

RW

p\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}r,t

ax

q\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}q

\delta_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}

q\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}t

RW

q\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}r,t

L\vee

p\vee q\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}r,t

with
{\bf T_{1}}=\{\{p^{\sf f}\}\}
,
{\bf S_{1}}=\{\{\neg q^{\sf f}\}\}
,
{\bf T_{2}}=\{\{q^{\sf f}\}\}
and
{\bf S_{2}}=\{\{\neg s^{\sf f}\}\}
. On the other hand, the immediate subderivations of
\pi
are proofs.

Example 3.3
.

Let
\mathcal{W}=\varnothing
and
\mathcal{D}
be defined as
\Big\{\dfrac{p:q}{r}\,;\,\dfrac{q:s}{\neg r}\Big\}
. The following derivation
\pi
in the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculus for
\langle\mathcal{W},\mathcal{D}\rangle
is a paraproof:

ax

p\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p

LW

p,q\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p

\delta_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}

p,q\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}r

ax

q\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}q

LW

p,q\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}q

\delta_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}

p,q\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\neg r

R\wedge

p,q\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}r\wedge\neg r

with
{\bf T_{1}}=\{\{p^{\sf f}\}\}
,
{\bf S_{1}}=\{\{\neg q^{\sf f}\}\}
,
{\bf T_{2}}=\{\{q^{\sf f}\}\}
and
{\bf S_{2}}=\{\{\neg s^{\sf f}\}\}
.
On the other hand, the immediate subderivations of
\pi
are proofs.

Before turning to the structural analysis of
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculi, let us record a few additional remarks concerning the conditions in Definition
3.2
.

(A)

For any extra-logical rule, the formulas that are principal in its applications may occur only on the right-hand side of the sequent. Replacing
\mathsf{top_{r}^{\#}}
with
\mathsf{top_{cp}^{\#}}
would therefore lead to unsound formalizations of default rules. Consider, for example, the default rule

\dfrac{\top:\neg p}{\neg p}.

The corresponding extra-logical rule would be

\Gamma,p\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbf{S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbf{T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}

with
\mathbf{T}=\{\{\top^{\sf f}\}\}
and
\mathbf{S}=\{\{\neg\neg p^{\sf f}\}\}
. For any context
\Gamma
, this rule produces an unsound conclusion: adopting it would make every derivation of
\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbf{S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbf{T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\neg p
have a paraproof as an immediate subderivation. The use of
\mathsf{top_{r}^{\#}}
is legitimate only because we work in the calculus
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G4^{pn}}}}
for classical logic, where negation is primitive and no rule moves a formula between the two sides of a sequent.

(B)

We use the term
semi-analytic Cut
for Cut applications where (a) both Cut formulas occur on the right-hand side, and (b) the Cut formula is a subformula of the formulas on the left-hand side of the conclusion or of the extra-logical axioms. Condition
(iii)
guarantees that
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculi contain atomic, semi-analytic Cut as a primitive rule. This is required for strong adequacy (cf. Theorem
3.8
), since
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculi without
cut_{asa}
are not closed under such applications. Consider e.g. the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculus for
\langle\mathcal{W},\mathcal{D}\rangle
, with

\mathcal{W}=\varnothing

\mathcal{D}=\Big\{\dfrac{\top:p}{p}\,,\,\dfrac{p\vee q:\neg p}{\neg p}\Big\}

Now, take the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof
\pi
:

ax

p\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p

RW

p\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p,q

ax

q\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}q

RW

q\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p,q

L\vee

p\vee q\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p,q

ax

p\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p

RW

p\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p,q

ax

q\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}q

RW

q\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p,q

L\vee

p\vee q\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p,q

\delta_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime\prime}}

p\vee q\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.11671pt\hskip-14.11671pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 14.11671pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-14.11671pt\raisebox{-8.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\{\neg\neg p\}$}}\hskip-14.11671pt\hskip 14.11671pt}$}\hskip-14.11671pt\hskip-13.93597pt\raisebox{11.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\{p\vee q\}$}}\hskip-13.93597pt\hskip 14.11671pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\neg p

cut_{asa}

p\vee q\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 14.11671pt\hskip-14.11671pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 14.11671pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-14.11671pt\raisebox{-8.5pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\{\neg\neg p\}$}}\hskip-14.11671pt\hskip 14.11671pt}$}\hskip-14.11671pt\hskip-13.93597pt\raisebox{11.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\{p\vee q\}$}}\hskip-13.93597pt\hskip 14.11671pt}\mkern 2.0mu}q

It is easy to verify that closure conditions
(vi)
-
(vii)
in Definition
3.2
are insufficient to absorb this Cut application.

(C)

Let us call a semi-analytic Cut
analytic
when the Cut formula is a subformula of the formulas on the left-hand side of the conclusion. If
\mathsf{top_{cp}^{\#}}
in condition (i) of Definition
3.2
is replaced by
\mathsf{top_{r}^{\#}}
, one can show that every non-analytic instance of
cut_{asa}
can be eliminated. By Theorems
3.3
,
3.4
, and
3.5
, it follows that
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculi are non-analytic cut-free complete. To eliminate analytic Cuts, one might consider imposing the following closure condition on the set of extra-logical rules. For any rule

\{\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.48051pt\hskip-3.85413pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.85413pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.85413pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbf{S_{i}}$}}\hskip-3.85413pt\hskip 3.85413pt}$}\hskip-3.85413pt\hskip-4.48051pt\raisebox{6.86388pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbf{T_{i}}$}}\hskip-4.48051pt\hskip 4.48051pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbf{S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbf{T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Phi

if a formula
\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\Phi^{\prime}
occurs in
\mathsf{top_{r}^{\#}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}(\bigvee\Phi)\wedge\bigwedge\Gamma)
but not in
\mathsf{top_{r}^{\#}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\bigvee\Phi)
nor in
\mathsf{top_{r}^{\#}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\bigwedge\Gamma)
, then one would also require the presence of the rule

\{\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.48051pt\hskip-3.85413pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.85413pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.85413pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbf{S_{i}}$}}\hskip-3.85413pt\hskip 3.85413pt}$}\hskip-3.85413pt\hskip-4.48051pt\raisebox{6.86388pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbf{T_{i}}$}}\hskip-4.48051pt\hskip 4.48051pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbf{S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbf{T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Phi^{\prime}

Yet this closure condition would generate an
infinite
collection of extra-logical rules, a situation we regard as highly undesirable.

3.3.
Structural properties

In this subsection, we carry out a structural analysis of the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculi for default theories in order to establish strong adequacy with respect to
m
-credulous consequence. To this end, we introduce some terminology.

A rule application is said to be
safe
when the existence of proofs for its premises guarantees the existence of a proof for its conclusion. A rule is
admissible
whenever

(i)

all its instances are safe;

(ii)

the set of conditions associated with the conclusion is included in the union of the sets of conditions associated with the premises; and

(iii)

the set of constraints of the premises coincides with that of the conclusion.

Moreover, a rule is said to be
invertible
when each inverse version of the rule is admissible.

Lemma 3.2
.

The rules
R\wedge,R\vee,R\neg\wedge,R\neg\vee
and
R\neg\neg
are invertible in
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
.

Proof.

We reason by induction on the height of a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof
\pi
of the premise(s). Let us focus on the rule
R\wedge
, since the others are analogous. Moreover, let’s take
B\wedge C
to be the active formula in the premise of the inverse of
R\wedge
.

If
h(\pi)=1
, the claim holds vacuously. Otherwise, we reason by cases over the last rule of
\pi
. If the last rule is
R\wedge
with principal formula
B\wedge C
, we take the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proofs of the premises of the latter, possibly followed by
\sigma
steps. Notice that the height may increase, but the
\sigma
-steps preserve correctness, so the result is still a proof. If the last rule is
RW
with principal
B\wedge C
, we replace that weakening with one yielding either
B
or
C
to obtain the desired conclusion. If
B\wedge C
is not principal in the last rule, apply the inductive hypothesis to the premises to obtain
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivations of the conclusions of the inverse rules. Applying the hypothesis above the last sequent in
\pi
preserves soundness, so each resulting
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivation is again a proof, as required.
∎

Lemma 3.3
.

The rule of Right Contraction

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,A,A

RC

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.4927pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-4.4927pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-4.4927pt\hskip 4.4927pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,A

is admissible in
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
.

Proof.

We proceed by primary induction on the height of the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof
\pi
of the premise and secondary induction on the logical complexity of
A
. If
h(\pi)=1
, conditions
(i)
and
(v)
of Definition
3.2
yield the conclusion, since
\mathsf{top_{cp}^{\#}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}B)
is closed under Contraction for every formula
B
. Otherwise, we analyze the last rule of
\pi
. If
A
is atomic and both occurrences are principal, we apply conditions
(v)
-
(vii)
of Definition
3.2
. If exactly one atomic occurrence of
A
is principal, the latter must be introduced by
RW
, and taking the premise of that
RW
instance suffices. If neither occurrence is principal, we apply the inductive hypothesis to the premises to permute
RC
upwards; this remains possible even in the presence of
cut_{asa}
, due to its context-sharing formulation. Soundness is preserved, as the left-hand side of the sequents is unchanged. If
A
is non-atomic, the only non-trivial cases occur when one occurrence of
A
is principal in the last rule. To handle these, we use Lemma
3.2
: since right-rule invertibility does not preserve height, the secondary inductive hypothesis is required to obtain the conclusion.
∎

Lemma 3.4
.

The rules
L\wedge,L\neg\vee
and
L\neg\neg
are invertible in
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
.

Proof.

We reason by induction on the height of a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof
\pi
of the premise. We argue as in the proof of Lemma
3.2
.
∎

Lemma 3.5
.

The logical rules of
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
are height-preserving invertible under the empty set of constraints.

Proof.

We reason by induction on the height of a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof
\pi
of the premise. We argue as in the proof of Lemma
3.2
: since we deal with empty sets of constraints and conditions, the height of the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof of the conclusion of the inverse rule does not surpass the height of the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proofs of the premise.
∎

Lemma 3.6
.

The rule of Left Contraction

A,A,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

LC

A,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

is admissible in
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
under the empty set of constraints.

Proof.

We reason by induction on the height of the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof
\pi
of the premise. As usual, we exploit Lemma
3.5
whenever one of the two occurrences of the contracted formula is principal in the last rule in
\pi
and the latter is a logical rule. Notice that we do not need secondary induction over the logical complexity of
A
, due to the fact invertibility is height-preserving under the empty sets of constraints.
∎

Theorem 3.1
.

The rule of Cut

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}A,\Delta

A,\Pi\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma

Cut

\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,\Sigma

is admissible in
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
.

Proof.

We argue as in

]
and

]

∎

Proposition 3.1
.

The Left and Right Negation rules

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,A

L\neg

\neg A,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

A,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

R\neg

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,\neg A

are admissible and invertible in
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
.

3.1
.
∎

Theorem 3.2
.

\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
proves
\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
if and only if

belongs to

.

Proof.

We argue as in
[
33
]
and
[
30
]
.
∎

Example 3.4
.

:
