Theorem 3.3
.

Let
A
be an atom occurring in some formula in
\Gamma,\Pi,\mathcal{W}
. The rule of safe, atomic semi-analytic Cut

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,A

\Pi\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma,\neg A

cut_{a}

\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,\Sigma

is admissible in
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
.

Proof.

For any such Cut application, consider the following
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivation:

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,A

LW

\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,A

RW

\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,\Sigma,A

\Pi\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma,\neg A

LW

\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma,\neg A

RW

\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,\Sigma,\neg A

cut_{asa}

\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,\Sigma

By hypothesis, all
LW
applications are safe: hence, such
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivation is a proof.
∎

Definition 3.5
.

Let
\pi
be a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivation of
\Gamma\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
and
A
be a formula occurrence in
\Gamma
(
\Delta
). The predecessors tree of
A
in
\pi
, in symbols
\mathcal{T}_{\pi}(A)
is a labelled tree generated according to the following procedure.

(i)

The root of
\mathcal{T}_{\pi}(A)
is labelled by the occurrence of
A
in
\Gamma
(
\Delta
).

(ii)

Let
\nu
be a node of
\mathcal{T}_{\pi}(A)
is labelled by an occurrence of
A
in the antecedent
\Pi
(succedent
\Sigma
, respectively) of a sequent
\Pi\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.4927pt\hskip-3.86632pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.86632pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.86632pt\raisebox{-6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-3.86632pt\hskip 3.86632pt}$}\hskip-3.86632pt\hskip-4.4927pt\raisebox{6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-4.4927pt\hskip 4.4927pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma
occurring in
\pi
, and
\star
be the last rule in the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-subderivation of
\pi
concluding
\Pi\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.4927pt\hskip-3.86632pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.86632pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.86632pt\raisebox{-6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-3.86632pt\hskip 3.86632pt}$}\hskip-3.86632pt\hskip-4.4927pt\raisebox{6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-4.4927pt\hskip 4.4927pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma
.

(ii.i)

If the occurrence of
A
is principal in
\star
,
\nu
has no immediate children.

(ii.ii)

If the occurrence of
A
is not principal in
\star
,
\nu
has (at most) two immediate children, and their labels are the instances of
A
occurring in the antecedents (succedents, respectively) of the premises of
\star
which correspond to the occurrence of
A
.

Theorem 3.4
.

Let
A
be an atom which does not occur in any formula in
\Gamma,\Pi,\mathcal{W}
. The rule of safe, atomic non-semi-analytic Cut

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,A

\Pi\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma,\neg A

cut_{a}

\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,\Sigma

is admissible in
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
.

Proof.

We focus on the topmost safe, atomic non-semi-analytic Cut application, reasoning by induction on the sum of the heights of the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proofs
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}
of the left and right premise, respectively. In the base case,
h(\pi_{1})=h(\pi_{2})=1
: since
A
does not occur in any formula in
\mathcal{W}
, it cannot be the case that last rule in
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}
is an extra-logical instance of
ax
. Moreover, if the last rule in
\pi_{1}
or
\pi_{2}
is a logical instance of
ax
, we leverage suitable
LW
and
cut_{asa}
applications to conclude. Hence, the only non-trivial scenario arises whenever the last rules in
\pi_{1}
and
\pi_{2}
are extra-logical ones: we leverage condition
(vii)
of Definition
3.2
to conclude. To prove the induction step, we reason by cases over the last rule in
\pi_{1}
.

If
A
is principal in the last rule of
\pi_{1}
, the only non-trivial cases occur when that last rule is extra-logical. To complete the proof, we then distinguish subcases according to the last rule in
\pi_{2}
.

(a)

If
\neg A
is principal in the last rule in
\pi_{2}
, the latter is either
RW
or an extra-logical rule
\delta
. In the first scenario, we apply
LW
,
RW
and
\sigma
to obtain a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivation of
\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,\Sigma
: it is easy to check that this derivation is a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof. In the second scenario, we leverage condition
(vii)
of Definition
3.2
to reach the conclusion: again, the application of
\delta
is safe by construction.

(b)

If
\neg A
is not principal in the last rule in
\pi_{2}
and latter is neither
L\vee
nor
L\neg\wedge
, we simply apply the inductive hypothesis to its premises to permute upwards the Cut application: notice that soundness under constraints is always preserved. If the last rule in
\pi_{2}
is either
L\vee
or
L\neg\wedge
, the same upwards permutation of Cut may fail, due to the fact that soundness under constraints may not be preserved. For instance, consider the following configuration – where
B^{f}
belongs to
\bigcup{\bf S_{1}}
,
\Pi=\Pi^{\prime},B\vee C
,
{\bf T_{2}}={\bf V_{1}}\cup{\bf V_{2}}
and
{\bf S_{2}}={\bf U_{1}}\cup{\bf U_{2}}
:

\vdots

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,A

\vdots

B,\Pi^{\prime}\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.593pt\hskip-5.593pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.593pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.593pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf U_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.593pt\hskip 5.593pt}$}\hskip-5.593pt\hskip-5.53328pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bf V_{1}$}}\hskip-5.53328pt\hskip 5.593pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma,\neg A

\vdots

C,\Pi^{\prime}\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.593pt\hskip-5.593pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.593pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.593pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf U_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.593pt\hskip 5.593pt}$}\hskip-5.593pt\hskip-5.53328pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bf V_{2}$}}\hskip-5.53328pt\hskip 5.593pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma,\neg A

L\vee

B\vee C,\Pi^{\prime}\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\bf T_{2}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma,\neg A

cut_{a}

B\vee C,\Pi^{\prime},\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,\Sigma

To reach the conclusion, we consider
\mathcal{T}_{\pi_{2}}(\neg A)
. If the label of any leaf in
\mathcal{T}_{\pi_{2}}(\neg A)
is introduced by an
RW
application, we remove such
RW
applications altogether, thus obtaining a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof
\pi^{\prime}_{2}
of
\Pi\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma
: we apply
LW
,
RW
and
\sigma
to conclude. Otherwise, there must exist one extra-logical rule in
\pi_{2}
. By contradiction, suppose this is not the case: from the fact that
A
does not occur in
\mathcal{W}
we infer the existence of (at least) one leaf in
\mathcal{T}_{\pi_{2}}(\neg A)
labelled by an occurrence of
\neg A
introduced by a logical instance of
ax
. This implies that
\neg A
occurs in
\Pi
– contrary to the hypothesis.

In the case where at least one extra-logical rule is applied in
\pi_{2}
, we first rewrite
\pi_{2}
globally and then proceed by secondary induction on the number of logical rules occurring below the topmost extra-logical rule applications.
Starting from these topmost applications, the soundness of
\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,\Sigma
guarantees that
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G4^{pn}}}}
proves
W,\Pi\vdash B
, where
B
is any premise of the corresponding default rule. Hence
B\in Cn(\mathcal{W}\cup\Pi)
. By Theorem
3.2
,
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
proves
\Pi\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta_{i}
for every
\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\Theta_{i}
in
\mathsf{top_{r}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}B)
.
We can now apply the extra-logical rule to the premises
\Pi\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta_{i}
; soundness of
\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,\Sigma
ensures that this application is safe. Next, we reapply all right rules that occur below this extra-logical rule in
\pi_{2}
. Instances of
cut_{asa}
are preserved, and all resulting sequents remain sound.
We iterate this procedure for each subsequent extra-logical rule in
\pi_{2}
. The resulting
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof
\pi_{2}^{\prime}
derives
\Pi\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma,\neg A
with strictly fewer logical rules below the highest extra-logical rule. At this point, the secondary inductive hypothesis applies, and the argument is complete.

If
A
is not principal in the last rule of
\pi_{1}
, and this rule is neither
L\vee
nor
L\neg\wedge
, we apply the inductive hypothesis (at most twice) to obtain the desired conclusion. If the last rule in
\pi_{1}
is, say,
L\vee
, we focus on
\mathcal{T}_{\pi_{1}}(A)
. If every leaf of
\mathcal{T}_{\pi_{1}}(A)
is labelled by an occurrence of
A
introduced by
RW
, we simply remove those
RW
applications to obtain a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof of
\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
, and then apply
LW
,
RW
, and
\sigma
to conclude. Otherwise, we reason by contradiction – as in case (b) for
\pi_{2}
– to obtain the existence of at least one extra-logical rule applied in
\pi_{1}
. We then rewrite
\pi_{1}
globally (again as in (b)) to reduce the number of logical rules occurring below the topmost extra-logical applications, and proceed by secondary induction on that number to complete the proof.
∎

Lemma 3.7
.

Let
i=1,2
. The rules

A_{i},A_{1}\vee A_{2},\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

E\vee

A_{i},A_{i},\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.4927pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-4.4927pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-4.4927pt\hskip 4.4927pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

\neg A_{i},\neg(A_{1}\wedge A_{2}),\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

E\neg\wedge

\neg A_{i},\neg A_{i},\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.4927pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-4.4927pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-4.4927pt\hskip 4.4927pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

are admissible in
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
.

Proof.

We focus on
E\vee
, since the argument for
E\neg\wedge
is completely analogous. We reason by induction on the height of the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof
\pi
of the premise. We assume that
{\bf S},{\bf T}\not=\varnothing
: if
{\bf S}={\bf T}=\varnothing
, we apply Lemma
3.5
to get the conclusion.

If
h(\pi)=1
, the last rule is a
0
-ary extra-logical rule: we simply replace
A_{1}\vee A_{2}
with
A_{i}
to obtain the conclusion. If
h(\pi)>1
, we reason by cases over the last rule in
\pi
. If the latter is
L\vee
and
A_{1}\vee A_{2}
is principal in it, we perform the following transformation on
\pi
:

\vdots

A_{i},A_{i},\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

\vdots

A_{i},A_{3-i},\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

L\vee

A_{i},A_{1}\vee A_{2},\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

\rightsquigarrow

\vdots

A_{i},A_{i},\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

\sigma

A_{i},A_{i},\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

If the last rule in
\pi
is
LW
and
A_{1}\vee A_{2}
is principal in it, we perform the following transformation:

\vdots

A_{i},\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

LW

A_{i},A_{1}\vee A_{2},\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

\rightsquigarrow

\vdots

A_{i},\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

LW

A_{i},A_{i},\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

If a formula in
\Gamma
or
\Delta
is principal in the last rule in
\pi
, we apply the inductive hypothesis to the premises of the latter to conclude.

If the last rule in
\pi
is
cut_{asa}
, the application of the inductive hypothesis to the premises may not be possible: if the atomic cut formula is a subformula of
A_{3-i}
without occurring in any formula in
\Gamma\cup[A_{i}]\cup\mathcal{W}
, the side condition for the applicability of
cut_{asa}
is no more satisfied. In this scenario, we perform the upwards permutation of
E\vee
: next, we replace the
cut_{asa}
application with an instance of
cut_{a}
. It suffices to exploit Theorem
3.4
to reach the conclusion.

If
A_{i}
is principal in the last rule in
\pi
, we cannot apply the inductive hypothesis to reach the conclusion: instead, we follow another argument, based on the fact that the premise
A_{i},A_{1}\vee A_{2},\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
is sound under conditions. Definition
3.3
ensures that
([W,E_{\rho}]\cup[A_{i}]\cup[A_{1}\vee A_{2}]\cup\Gamma)\,||\,\langle{\bf V},\varnothing\rangle
for any subderivation
\rho
of
\pi
with
\Pi\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.4472pt\hskip-3.4472pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.4472pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.4472pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf U}$}}\hskip-3.4472pt\hskip 3.4472pt}$}\hskip-3.4472pt\hskip-3.3875pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf V}$}}\hskip-3.3875pt\hskip 3.4472pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma
as conclusion. Starting from the topmost applications of extra-logical rules in
\pi
, if
\Theta
is the succedent of any premise of such applications, we leverage Theorem
3.2
to infer that
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
proves
A_{i},A_{i},\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta
. Hence, we apply the corresponding extra-logical rules to these sequents: since
([W,D_{\pi}]\cup[A_{i}]\cup[A_{1}\vee A_{2}]\cup\Gamma)\,||\,\langle\varnothing,{\bf S}\rangle
, Lemma
3.1
ensures that such applications are safe. Next, we apply all the rules applied in
\pi
below such extra-logical rules instances, with the exception of left (logical and structural) rules. Theorem
3.4
ensures that any application of
cut_{asa}
which is not preserved by the deletion of
A_{3-i}
can be replaced by an instance of
cut_{a}
. Moreover, the fact that
([W,D_{\pi}]\cup[A_{i}]\cup[A_{1}\vee A_{2}]\cup\Gamma)\,||\,\langle\varnothing,{\bf S}\rangle
, together with Lemma
3.1
, guarantees that all
\sigma
applications remain safe. Such global rewriting of
\pi
yields a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof of
A_{i},A_{i},\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
.
∎

Lemma 3.8
.

The rule of Left Contraction

A,A,\Gamma\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

LC

A,\Gamma\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

is admissible in
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
.

Proof.

We focus on Left Contraction, arguing by primary induction on the height of the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof
\pi
of the premise and secondary induction on the logical complexity of
A
. If
h(\pi)=1
, we leverage conditions
(i)
and
(v)
in Definition
3.2
to reach the conclusion. If
h(\pi)>1
, we reason by cases over the last rule in
\pi
. If no occurrence of
A
is principal in the last rule , we simply apply the inductive hypothesis to the premises. If one occurrence of
A
is principal in the last rule , we proceed by cases over
A
’s principal connective. If
A
is a literal, then
A
is introduced by an
LW
application: it suffices to remove the latter to conclude. If
A
has either the form
B\wedge C
, or the form
\neg(B\vee C)
, or the form
\neg\neg B
, we leverage Lemma
3.4
to apply the secondary inductive hypothesis and reach the conclusion. On the other hand, if
A
has either the form
B\vee C
or the form
\neg(B\wedge C)
, we exploit Lemma
3.7
to apply twice the secondary inductive hypothesis and complete the proof.
∎

Theorem 3.5
.

The rule of safe Cut

\Gamma\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,A

\Pi\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma,\neg A

cut

\Gamma,\Pi\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta,\Sigma

is admissible in
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
.

Proof.

We reason by induction over the complexity of
A
. If
A
is a literal, Theorems
3.3
and
3.4
ensure the conclusion. If
A
is not a literal, we reason by cases over
A
’s principal connective. We leverage Lemma
3.2
to lower the complexity of the Cut formula: to reach the conclusion, we apply Lemmas
3.3
and
3.8
.
∎

\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculi are expressive enough to formalize weak cumulativity properties satisfied by modified credulous consequence. First, let us consider the following Cumulative Cut rule – a special case of the standard Cut rule:

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}A

A,\Pi\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma

cCut

\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma

Theorem 3.6
.

The rule of safe Cumulative Cut is admissible in
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
.

Proof.

We focus on a topmost Cumulative Cut application, reasoning by primary induction on the logical complexity of the Cut formula
A
and secondary induction on the sum of the heights of the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proofs
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}
of the left and right premise, respectively. In view of Theorem
3.1
, we assume that either
{\bf S}\not=\varnothing
or
{\bf T}\not=\varnothing
. If
A
is a literal, we reason by cases over the last rule in
\pi_{1}
. Here, we focus on the most meaningful cases.

(a)

If the last rule in
\pi_{1}
is
ax
, then by hypothesis, the last rule in
\pi_{2}
is not
ax
. If the last rule in
\pi_{2}
is extra-logical, apply the secondary inductive hypothesis
m
times to the premises to obtain the conclusion: if
m=0
, replace the
\delta
-application concluding
A,\Pi\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma
with one concluding
\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma
, (possibly) followed by
\sigma
-applications yielding
\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma
. It is easy to see that all rule applications in the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivation thus obtained are safe. If the last rule in
\pi_{2}
is
LW
and
A
principal in it, we replace the Weakening application with
LW
applications adding the formulas in
\Gamma
(if any), plus
\sigma
applications to add sets in
{\bf S}
not occurring in
{\bf S_{2}}
(if any). If the last rule in
\pi_{2}
is
cut_{asa}
, we apply the secondary inductive hypothesis to its premises: if the side condition for the applicability of
cut_{asa}
is not fulfilled, we leverage Theorem
3.4
to reach the conclusion. If the last rule in
\pi_{2}
is either
L\vee
or
L\neg\wedge
, we may not be able to apply the secondary inductive hypothesis without losing soundness under constraints. If no extra-logical rule is applied in
\pi_{2}
, we remove any
\sigma
application from
\pi_{2}
and apply Proposition
3.1
to infer the existence of a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof of
\Pi\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma,\neg A
. We apply
\sigma
to obtain a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivation of
\Pi\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.19167pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 4.65967pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma,\neg A
: it is immediate to verify that these
\sigma
-application are safe, and thus that such derivation is a proof. If there exists (at least) one extra-logical rule applied in
\pi_{2}
, we globally rewrite
\pi_{2}
to obtain a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof of
\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma
(following the same procedure as in the proof of Theorem
3.4
, point
(b)
).

(b)

If the last rule in
\pi_{1}
is extra-logical and the last rule in
\pi_{2}
is
ax
, we apply Lemma
3.1
and Theorem
3.5
to conclude. All other possible scenarios are treated as in
(a)
above.

(c)

If the last rule in
\pi_{1}
is either
L\vee
or
L\neg\wedge
, we may not be able to apply the secondary inductive hypothesis to both premises to reach the conclusion. Instead, we globally rewrite
\pi_{2}
to obtain a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof of
\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma
(again, following the same procedure as in the proof of Theorem
3.4
, point
(b)
).

If
A
is not a literal, we reason by cases over
A
’s principal connective. If
A
is either
B\wedge C
or
\neg(B\vee C)
or
\neg\neg B
, we leverage Lemmas
3.2
and
3.4
to apply (at most, twice) the primary inductive hypothesis, and then Lemmas
3.3
and
3.8
to reach the conclusion. If
A
is either
B\vee C
or
\neg(B\wedge C)
, and
\pi_{2}
does not contain applications of extra-logical rules, we remove all
\sigma
applications (if any) and then apply Lemma
3.1
and Theorem
3.5
to obtain a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof of
\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma
: we employ safe
\sigma
applications to prove
\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma
. On the other hand, if
\pi_{2}
contains applications of extra-logical rules, we globally rewrite
\pi_{2}
exploiting soundness under conditions to obtain a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof of
\Pi,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Sigma
(again, following the same procedure as in the proof of Theorem
3.4
, point
(b)
).

∎

Next, let us consider the following Cautious Monotony rule:

Theorem 3.7
.

The rule of safe Cautious Monotony

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta_{1}

\Pi\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta_{2}

cMon

\Gamma,\bigvee\Delta_{1},\Pi\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta_{2}

is admissible in
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
.

Proof.

We reason by induction over the sum of the heights of the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proofs
\pi_{1},\pi_{2}
of the left and right premise, respectively. If
h(\pi_{1})=h(\pi_{2})=1
, the only non-trivial case is when (say) the last rule in
\pi_{1}
is an extra-logical rule
\delta
. We then inspect the last rule of
\pi_{2}
. If it is
ax
, applying
LW
and
\sigma
yields a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivation of
\bigvee\Delta_{1},\Gamma,\Pi\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\mathbf{S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.19167pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.51389pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta_{2}
, which is easily seen to be a proof. If the last rule in
\pi_{2}
is an extra-logical
\delta^{\prime}
, we replace its application with one whose conclusion has
\bigvee\Delta_{1},\Gamma,\Pi
in the antecedent, and then apply
\sigma
; soundness under conditions is immediate, and soundness under constraints follows from Lemma
3.1
.

If
h(\pi_{1})+h(\pi_{2})>2
, we proceed by cases on the last rule of
\pi_{2}
. If a formula in
\Pi
is principal, the inductive hypothesis applies directly to the premises; the same reasoning covers the case where the last rule is
cut_{asa}
. If instead a formula in
\Delta_{2}
is principal and the last rule is an extra-logical
\delta
, we apply
LW
and
\sigma
to the premises of
\delta
, and then reapply
\delta
. By Lemma
3.1
, soundness under constraints is preserved, so the resulting
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivation is a proof.
∎

Now, we proceed to the proof of strong adequacy for
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculi – the main result of this section.

Theorem 3.8
.

Let
\langle\mathcal{W},\mathcal{D}\rangle
be a default theory. Then there exists a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof
\pi
of
\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
, for some
{\bf S}
and
{\bf T}
, iff
\bigvee\Delta
belongs to some modified extension of
\langle\mathcal{W}\cup\Gamma,\mathcal{D}\rangle
.

Proof.

(\Rightarrow)
We reason by induction on the height of
\pi
to establish the existence of a modified extension
\langle\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F}\rangle
of
\langle\mathcal{W}\cup\Gamma,def(\pi)\rangle
such that
\bigvee\Delta
belongs to
\mathcal{E}
: by Lemma
2.1
, this suffices to the conclusion.

If
h(\pi)=1
, the only non-trivial case is when the last rule is an extra-logical rule
\delta_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}
. By conditions
(v)-(vii)
in Definition
3.2
,
\bigvee\Delta
belongs to
Cn(D)
, where
D
is the conclusion of the single default rule in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
. From the fact that
\Gamma\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
is sound under constraints we infer that
\bigvee\Delta
belongs to a modified extension
\langle\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F}\rangle
of
\langle\mathcal{W}\cup\Gamma,\mathcal{D}^{\prime}\rangle
. If
h(\pi)>1
, we reason by cases over the last rule in
\pi
. If the latter is
RW
,
\sigma
or a unary logical rule, we apply the inductive hypothesis to the premises to reach the conclusion: for left logical rules, we leverage the fact that any modified extension of
\langle\mathcal{W}^{\prime},def(\pi)\rangle
is a modified extension of
\langle\mathcal{W}^{\prime\prime},def(\pi)\rangle
and vice versa, whenever
\mathcal{W}^{\prime}
is classically equivalent to
\mathcal{W}^{\prime\prime}
. If the last rule in
\pi
is
LW
, the formula
A
is principal in it and
\Gamma=\Gamma^{\prime}\cup[A]
, we apply the inductive hypothesis to infer the existence of a modified extension
\langle\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F}\rangle
of
\langle\mathcal{W}\cup\Gamma^{\prime},def(\pi)\rangle
: from the fact that
A,\Gamma^{\prime}\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
is sound under constraints we obtain the conclusion. If the last rule in
\pi
is an extra-logical rule
\delta_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}
and
\delta_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}
is generated according to point
(v)
in Definition
3.2
,
\pi
has the form:

\vdots_{\pi_{1}}

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta_{1}

\cdots

\vdots_{\pi_{m}}

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 6.49437pt\hskip-5.86798pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 5.86798pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-5.86798pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{m}}$}}\hskip-5.86798pt\hskip 5.86798pt}$}\hskip-5.86798pt\hskip-6.49437pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{m}}$}}\hskip-6.49437pt\hskip 6.49437pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta_{m}

\delta_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Phi

with
m>1
. By Definitions
3.1
and
3.3
, the fact that
\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Phi
is sound under conditions entails that
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G4^{pn}}}}
proves
W,D^{\prime}_{\pi},\Gamma\vdash\bigwedge_{i=1}^{m}(\bigvee\Theta_{i})
: by definition, this means that
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G4^{pn}}}}
proves
W,\bigwedge concl(def(\pi_{1})\cup\cdots\cup def(\pi_{m})),\Gamma\vdash\bigwedge_{i=1}^{m}(\bigvee\Theta_{i})
. On the other hand,
\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Phi
is sound under constraints: Definitions
3.1
,
3.3
and Lemma
3.1
ensure the existence of a modified extension
\langle\mathcal{E}^{\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime}\rangle
of
\langle\mathcal{W}\cup\Gamma,def(\pi_{1})\cup\cdots\cup def(\pi_{m})\rangle
such that
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}=Cn(\mathcal{W}\cup concl(def(\pi_{1})\cup\cdots\cup def(\pi_{m})))
. As a result, we have that
\bigwedge_{i=1}^{m}(\bigvee\Theta_{i})
belongs to
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
: hence, the default rule in
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
is triggered in
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
. The soundness of
\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Phi
under constraints guarantees the existence of a modified extension
\langle\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime},\mathcal{F}^{\prime\prime}\rangle
of
\langle\mathcal{W}\cup\Gamma,def(\pi)\rangle
such that the conclusion of the default rule belongs to
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}
: by condition
(v)
of Definition
3.2
and closure under
modus ponens
of
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}
we infer that
\bigvee\Phi
belongs to
\mathcal{E}^{\prime\prime}
.
The arguments are analogous when
\delta_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}
is generated according to points
(vi)
–
(vii)
. If the last rule in
\pi
is a binary right logical rule or
cut_{asa}
, we apply the inductive hypothesis twice and use the soundness of
\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
to derive the conclusion. If the last rule is
L\vee
or
L\neg\wedge
, then
\pi
takes the form:

\vdots

B,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

\vdots

C,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

L\vee

B\vee C,\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

If no extra-logical rule is applied in
\pi
, Theorem
3.2
ensures that
\bigvee\Delta
belongs to
Cn(\mathcal{W}\cup\Gamma)
, and thus to any modified extension of
\langle\mathcal{W}\cup\Gamma,\mathcal{D}\rangle
. If at least one extra-logical rule occurs in
\pi
, we globally rewrite
\pi
so as to permute
L\vee
above any extra-logical rule application, thus obtaining a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivation
\rho
. Then, we proceed by induction on the number of instances of extra-logical rules in
\rho
to conclude.

(\Leftarrow)
If
\langle\mathcal{E},\mathcal{F}\rangle
is a modified extension of
\langle\mathcal{W},\mathcal{D}\rangle
and
A\in\mathcal{E}
, then there exists a
k
such that
A\in\mathcal{E}^{k}
. We show by (transfinite) induction on
k
that there exists (at least) one
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof
\pi
of
\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}A
, for some
{\bf S},{\bf T}
, such that
D_{\pi}\in\mathcal{E}
. If
k=j+1
and
A
follows from formulas in
\mathcal{E}^{j}

via

modus ponens
, Theorem
3.5
s yields the conclusion. The crucial case is when
k=j+1
,
A
is not a classical consequence of formulas in
\mathcal{E}^{j}
and there exists a default rule of the form
\dfrac{B:C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}}{A}
such that
B\in\mathcal{E}^{j}
. If
A\in\mathcal{E}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\prime}
represents the maximal set of applicable defaults triggered by formulas in
\mathcal{E}^{j}
, then
C_{h}\in(\mathcal{F}\cup just(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}))
, and so
\neg C_{h}\not\in Cn(\mathcal{E}\cup concl(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}))
, for any
1\leq h\leq k
. By inductive hypothesis, there exists (at least) one
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof
\rho
of
\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.4472pt\hskip-3.4472pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.4472pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.4472pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf U}$}}\hskip-3.4472pt\hskip 3.4472pt}$}\hskip-3.4472pt\hskip-3.3875pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf V}$}}\hskip-3.3875pt\hskip 3.4472pt}\mkern 2.0mu}B
, for some
{\bf U},{\bf V}
, such that
D_{\rho}\in\mathcal{E}
. Theorem
3.5
and Lemma
3.3
guarantee the existence of a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof
\rho_{i}
of
\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.73993pt\hskip-3.4472pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.4472pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.4472pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf U}$}}\hskip-3.4472pt\hskip 3.4472pt}$}\hskip-3.4472pt\hskip-4.73993pt\raisebox{6.86388pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf V^{\prime}_{i}}$}}\hskip-4.73993pt\hskip 4.73993pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta_{i}
, for any
\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\Theta_{i}
occurring in
\mathsf{top_{r}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}B)=\{\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\Theta_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}
, such that
D_{\rho_{i}}\in\mathcal{E}
. Hence, for any
\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\Phi
in
\mathsf{top_{r}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}A)
consider the following
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivation
\pi^{\prime}
:

\vdots_{\rho_{i}}

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.73993pt\hskip-3.4472pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.4472pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.4472pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf U}$}}\hskip-3.4472pt\hskip 3.4472pt}$}\hskip-3.4472pt\hskip-4.73993pt\raisebox{6.86388pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf V^{\prime}_{i}}$}}\hskip-4.73993pt\hskip 4.73993pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta_{i}

\delta_{\mathcal{D}^{\prime}}

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.79965pt\hskip-4.79965pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.79965pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.79965pt\raisebox{-6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf U^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-4.79965pt\hskip 4.79965pt}$}\hskip-4.79965pt\hskip-4.73993pt\raisebox{6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf V^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-4.73993pt\hskip 4.79965pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Phi

where
{\bf U^{\prime}}={\bf U}\cup\{\{\neg C_{1}^{f},\ldots,\neg C_{k}^{f}\}\}
. If
\neg C_{h}\not\in Cn(\mathcal{E}\cup concl(\mathcal{D}^{\prime}))
, then
\neg C_{h}\not\in Cn(\mathcal{W}\cup\{D_{\rho_{i}}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}\cup\{A\})
: this implies that
\pi^{\prime}
is a
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof, as desired.
∎

3.4.
Control pairs for normative systems

In this subsection, we extend the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculi for
m
-credulous consequence by introducing rules for
{\sf O}
-labelled and
{\sf P}
-labelled sequents. This yields strongly complete
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculi for both
m
- and
d
-credulous consequence. These calculi will serve in the next section as the basis for analysing paradoxical or dilemmatic scenarios involving conflicting information (such as typicality-based obligations, contrary-to-duty obligations, specificity-driven reasoning, and obligations with exceptions), as well as for treating deontic notions in dynamic settings (including dynamic positive permissions and dynamic obligations).

In
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculi for
d
-credulous consequence, factual detachment is formalized
via
specific extra-logical rules, which have
{\sf F}
-labelled premises and
\times
-labelled conclusions, with
\times\in\{{\sf O},{\sf P}\}
. To keep trace of the
factual assumptions
on the left-hand side of the
{\sf F}
-labelled premises, we enrich the structure of each controlled
\times
-labelled sequent with an additional context, a
repository
. Formally, a
deontic controlled sequent
is a controlled sequent

\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf\times}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

with attached a context
\Pi
, as follows:

\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf\times}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta

The rules of
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
transmit repositories along derivations in the same way as they transmit control pairs (see the rules in Figure
3
).

Repositories were originally introduced in
[
28
]
to keep trace of the formulas shifted by the rules from the left-hand side of the sequent to the right-hand side, in order to ensure Cut elimination. In
[
34
]
, the authors show that repositories can be dropped
via
a polarity-based definition of soundness. In the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculi presented here, no rule licenses shifts of formulas from the left-hand side of the sequent to the right-hand side: as a result, there is no need of repositories to store negative formulas, as in
[
28
]
, and no need of a polarity-based definition of soundness, like in
[
34
]
. Rather, we employ repositories with the purpose of representing factual assumptions in parallel with deontic assumptions – i.e., the formulas on the left-hand side of
\times
-labelled sequents.

The provability of a sequent of the form (say)
\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
corresponds to the truth of the statement ‘in (at least) one modified extension of
\langle\mathcal{W}\cup\Pi,\mathcal{D}\rangle
,
\bigvee\Delta
is obligatory according to the normative system

\langle\langle\mathcal{W}^{\sf o}\cup\Gamma,\mathcal{O}\rangle,\langle\mathcal{W}^{\sf p},\mathcal{P}\rangle\rangle
’
4
4

4

We can see a modified extension of
\langle\mathcal{W}\cup\Pi,\mathcal{D}\rangle
as an incomplete possible world, and
\langle\langle\mathcal{W}^{\sf o}\cup\Gamma,\mathcal{O}\rangle,\langle\mathcal{W}^{\sf p},\mathcal{P}\rangle\rangle
as an incomplete normative system – i.e., a normative system such that there exists (at least) one formula which is neither explicitly required, nor explicitly permitted, nor explicitly prohibited by the system. Under this view, provability of
\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
would correspond to truth in
incomplete
versions of Gibbardian factual-normative worlds
[
12
]
.
. Let us note that labeling the turnstiles rather than the formulas they separate reflects the idea that a formula’s content remains the same regardless of whether it appears in a factual or a normative statement. The same formula can play different roles in different games -- the ‘game’ of facts, the ‘game’ of obligations, the ‘game’ of permissions -- just as a single card in a deck can assume different functions depending on the game being played
5
5

5

Formulas occurring in control pairs are labeled just for notational convenience.
.

extra-logical rules

\{\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.48051pt\hskip-3.85413pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.85413pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.85413pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{i}}$}}\hskip-3.85413pt\hskip 3.85413pt}$}\hskip-3.85413pt\hskip-4.48051pt\raisebox{6.86388pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{i}}$}}\hskip-4.48051pt\hskip 4.48051pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta_{i}\}_{0\leq i\leq m}

\delta

\delta

\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf\times}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Phi

\{\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.48051pt\hskip-3.85413pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.85413pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.85413pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{i}}$}}\hskip-3.85413pt\hskip 3.85413pt}$}\hskip-3.85413pt\hskip-4.48051pt\raisebox{6.86388pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{i}}$}}\hskip-4.48051pt\hskip 4.48051pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta_{i}\}_{0\leq i\leq m}

\{\Pi_{i}\mid\Delta\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf\times}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.58122pt\hskip-3.95483pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.95483pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.95483pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{j}}$}}\hskip-3.95483pt\hskip 3.95483pt}$}\hskip-3.95483pt\hskip-4.58122pt\raisebox{7.83609pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{j}}$}}\hskip-4.58122pt\hskip 4.58122pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta_{i}\}_{0\leq j\leq n}

\delta

\Gamma,\Pi\mid\Delta\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf\times}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Phi

Figure 3.
Controlled sequent rules for a normative system
\langle\langle\mathcal{W}^{{\sf o}},\mathcal{O}\rangle,\langle\mathcal{W}^{{\sf p}},\mathcal{P}\rangle\rangle

Definition 3.6
.

Let
\langle\mathcal{W},\mathcal{D}\rangle
,
\langle\mathcal{W}^{\sf o},\mathcal{O}\rangle
and
\langle\mathcal{W}^{\sf p},\mathcal{P}\rangle
be a default theory, a system of obligations and a system of permissions, respectively. The controlled sequent calculus
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
for
\langle\mathcal{W},\mathcal{D}\rangle
,
\langle\mathcal{W}^{\sf o},\mathcal{O}\rangle
and
\langle\mathcal{W}^{\sf p},\mathcal{P}\rangle
is an extension of the
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculus for
\langle\mathcal{W},\mathcal{D}\rangle
with rules in Figure
3
, provided that the conditions below are fulfilled.

(i_{op})

For each instance of
ax^{\times}
, one of the following conditions holds:
\Theta=\Lambda=\{A\}
for some literal
A
;
\Theta=\{A,\neg A\}
or
\Lambda=\{A,\neg A\}
for some atom
A
;
\Theta\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\Lambda
belongs to
\mathsf{top_{cp}^{\#}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}W^{\times})
, with
W^{\times}
being the conjunction of the formulas in
\mathcal{W}^{\times}
.

(ii_{op})

For each structural and logical rule, the left subscript of the turnstile
\mathrel{\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{1.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt}\mkern 2.0mu}
and the right superscript of the rule name,
\times
, is either
{\sf o}
or
{\sf p}
.

(iii_{op})

For any constrained conditional obligation
\dfrac{B:C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}}{D}
in
\mathcal{O}^{\sf f}
, if
\mathsf{top_{r}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}B)=\{\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\Theta_{1},\ldots,\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\Theta_{m}\}
with
m>0
and
\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\Phi
is in
\mathsf{top_{r}^{\#}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}D)
, then there exists an extra-logical rule:

\{\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.48051pt\hskip-3.85413pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.85413pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.85413pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{i}}$}}\hskip-3.85413pt\hskip 3.85413pt}$}\hskip-3.85413pt\hskip-4.48051pt\raisebox{6.86388pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{i}}$}}\hskip-4.48051pt\hskip 4.48051pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Theta_{i}\}_{1\leq i\leq m}

\delta

\Gamma\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Phi

where
{\bf T}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}{\bf T_{i}}\cup\{\{B^{{\sf f}}\}\}
,
{\bf S}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}{\bf S_{i}}\cup\{\{\neg C_{1}^{{\sf o}},\ldots,\neg C_{k}^{{\sf o}}\}\}
. If instead
\mathsf{top_{r}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}B)=\varnothing
, then there exists an extra-logical rule:

\delta

\Pi\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Phi

with
{\bf T}=\{\{\top^{{\sf f}}\}\}
and
{\bf S}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}{\bf S_{i}}\cup\{\{\neg C_{1}^{{\sf o}},\ldots,\neg C_{k}^{{\sf o}}\}\}
.

The same condition applies to any constrained conditional permission in
\mathcal{P}^{\sf f}
, replacing
\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{1.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt}\mkern 2.0mu}
with
\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf P}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{1.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt}\mkern 2.0mu}
, and
{\bf S}=\bigcup_{i=1}^{m}\mathbf{S_{i}}\cup\{\{\neg C_{1}^{\sf p},\ldots,\neg C_{k}^{\sf p},\neg C_{1}^{\sf o},\ldots,\neg C_{k}^{\sf o}\}\}
.

(iv_{op})

For any constrained conditional obligation
\dfrac{B:C_{1},\ldots,C_{k}}{D}
in
\mathcal{O}^{\sf d}
, if
\mathsf{top_{r}}(\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}B)=\{\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\Theta_{1},\ldots,\scriptsize\mathrel{\hbox{\raisebox{2.41112pt}{$\rule[-4.52083pt]{0.33452pt}{9.04166pt}\rule[-0.16725pt]{5.7277pt}{0.33452pt}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{-1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{}$}}}\hskip-5.7277pt\raisebox{1.37277pt}{\makebox[5.7277pt]{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\ast}$}}}$}}}\Theta_{m}\}
with
