. Such derivation is a proof. This corresponds to the fact that if the obligation of not double-parking is actual, then the obligation of paying a fine is actual.

Example 4.10

(Guarded free choice permission)
.

Let the extra-logical axiom and rule

ax^{\sf P}

\Pi\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf P}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p,q,\neg r

\delta_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}}

\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}r

stand for the unconditional permission ‘One is permitted to work or relax or not pay the bill’ and the unconditional obligation ‘One ought to pay the bill’, respectively – where
{\bf T_{1}}=\{\{\top^{\sf f}\}\}
and
{\bf S_{1}}=\{\{\neg r^{\sf o}\}\}
.

The free choice principle allows to infer that a formula
A_{i}
is permitted whenever
A_{1}\vee\cdots\vee A_{m}
is permitted, for any
1\leq i\leq m

[
45
, p. 21]
. Even when limiting ourselves to consider disjunctions obtained without any application of
RW
, unrestricted application of the free choice principle leads to permission explosion
[
3
]
. In our scenario, the free choice principle ensures the existence of a permission not to pay the bill, whereas the obligation to pay the bill entails the permission to pay the bill (since
\mathcal{O}\subseteq\mathcal{P}
in the underlying normative system).

The unrestricted free choice principle over unconstrained permissions is captured by closing
{\sf P}
-labelled extra-logical axioms under the rule

\Pi\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf P}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}A_{1},\ldots,A_{m}

fcp

\Pi\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf P}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}A_{i}

To block undesired permissions, we close
{\sf P}
-labelled extra-logical axioms under the rule

\Pi\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf P}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}A_{1},\ldots,A_{m}

fcp

\Pi\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf P}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.19167pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.51389pt}\mkern 2.0mu}A_{i}

where
{\bf S}=\{\{\neg O_{u},\neg O_{c}\}\}
, with
O_{u}
being the conjunction of all formulas in
\mathcal{W}^{\sf o}
and
O_{c}
being the conjunction of all formulas in
concl(\mathcal{O})
.

Example 4.11

(Permissions as exceptions)
.

Let the extra-logical rule

\delta_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}}

\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\neg p

stand for the unconditional obligation ‘One ought not kill herself’ – where
{\bf T}=\{\{\top^{\sf f}\}\}
and
{\bf S}=\{\{\neg\neg p^{\sf o}\}\}
. The Torah contains such obligation as a commandment (cf. Genesis 9:5). However, some Rabbinic scholars state that suicide (or surrender to death) is permissible when one is threatened with conversion (as in the case of King Saul in 2 Samuel, 1:5-10). Let the extra-logical rule

\delta_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.86632pt\hskip-3.86632pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.86632pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.86632pt\raisebox{-6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-3.86632pt\hskip 3.86632pt}$}\hskip-3.86632pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.86632pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\neg p

stand for the unconditional obligation with exceptions ‘One ought not kill herself, provided that she is not threatened with conversion’ – where
{\bf S^{\prime}}={\bf S}\cup\{\{q^{\sf f}\}\}
. To infer the positive permission to kill oneself, we can apply the following rule
pe

\delta_{\mathcal{O}^{\prime}}^{\prime}

\varnothing\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.86632pt\hskip-3.86632pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.86632pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.86632pt\raisebox{-6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-3.86632pt\hskip 3.86632pt}$}\hskip-3.86632pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.86632pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\neg p

pe_{\mathcal{W}^{\sf p\star}}

q\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf P}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p

with
\mathcal{W}^{\sf p\star}=\Big\{\dfrac{\top:\top}{\neg p}\Big\}
.

Example 4.12

(Dynamic positive permissions)
.

The notion of dynamic positive permission is introduced in
[
23
]
:
B
is dynamically permitted under a condition
A
if prohibiting
B
in the context of
A
would block the exercise of some explicit (static) permission, thereby generating incoherence.
Let the extra-logical rules

\delta_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}

\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf P}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p

\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\neg q

\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}

\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S^{\prime}_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{7.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T^{\prime}_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\neg p

stand for the unconditional permission ‘One is permitted to express herself freely’ and the conditional obligation ‘If one ought not print cartoons depicting Mohammed, then one ought not express herself freely’, respectively – where

(i)

{\bf T_{1}}=\{\{\top^{\sf f}\}\}
and
{\bf S_{1}}=\{\{\neg p^{\sf p}\}\}
;

(ii)

{\bf T^{\prime}_{2}}={\bf T_{2}}\cup\{\{\neg q^{\sf o}\}\}
and
{\bf S^{\prime}_{2}}={\bf S_{2}}\cup\{\{\neg\neg p^{\sf o}\}\}
.

Suppose one adds to
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
the extra-logical rule

\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{2}}

\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.4472pt\hskip-3.4472pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.4472pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.4472pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf U}$}}\hskip-3.4472pt\hskip 3.4472pt}$}\hskip-3.4472pt\hskip-3.3875pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf V}$}}\hskip-3.3875pt\hskip 3.4472pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\neg q

standing for the unconditional obligation ‘One ought not print the cartoons depicting Mohammed’ – where
{\bf V}=\{\{\top^{\sf f}\}\}
and
{\bf U}=\{\{\neg\neg q^{\sf o}\}\}
. The derivation of the dynamic permission to print the cartoons depicting Mohammed corresponds to the following application of a suitable inference rule
dp
:

\delta_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}

\varnothing\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf P}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p

\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{2}}

\varnothing\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.4472pt\hskip-3.4472pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 3.4472pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-3.4472pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf U}$}}\hskip-3.4472pt\hskip 3.4472pt}$}\hskip-3.4472pt\hskip-3.3875pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf V}$}}\hskip-3.3875pt\hskip 3.4472pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\neg q

\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}

\varnothing\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 4.79965pt\hskip-4.79965pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.79965pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.79965pt\raisebox{-6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf U^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-4.79965pt\hskip 4.79965pt}$}\hskip-4.79965pt\hskip-4.73993pt\raisebox{6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf V^{\prime}}$}}\hskip-4.73993pt\hskip 4.79965pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\neg p

dp_{\mathcal{W}^{\sf p\star}}

\varnothing\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf P}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}q

with
{\bf V^{\prime}}={\bf V}\cup\{\{\neg q^{\sf o}\}\}
,
{\bf U^{\prime}}={\bf U}\cup\{\{\neg\neg p^{\sf o}\}\}
,
{\bf T}={\bf T_{1}}\cup{\bf V}
,
{\bf S}={\bf S_{1}}
and
\mathcal{W}^{\sf p\star}=\Big\{\dfrac{\top:\top}{q}\Big\}
. Notice that neither
{\bf T}
is
{\bf T_{1}}\cup{\bf V^{\prime}}
, nor
{\bf S}
is
{\bf S_{1}}\cup{\bf U^{\prime}}
. In other words, the
dp
application causes the removal of sets of formulas from
{\bf T_{1}}\cup{\bf V^{\prime}}
and
{\bf S_{1}}\cup{\bf U^{\prime}}
.

Example 4.13

(Talmudic
Qal wa-ḥomer
)
.

Let the extra-logical rules

\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}

\Pi\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\neg p

\delta_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}

\Pi\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf P}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}q

\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf P}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{3}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{3}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}q

\delta_{\mathcal{P}_{2}}

\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf P}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S^{\prime}_{3}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{7.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T^{\prime}_{3}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p

stand for the unconditional obligation ‘One ought not marry the daughter of his daughter’, the unconditional permission ‘One is allowed to marry one’s daughter’ and the conditional permission ‘If one is allowed to marry one’s daughter, one is allowed to marry the daughter of his daughter’, respectively – where

(i)

{\bf T_{1}}=\{\{\top^{\sf o}\}\}
and
{\bf S_{1}}=\{\{\neg\neg p^{\sf o}\}\}
;

(ii)

{\bf T_{2}}=\{\{\top^{\sf p}\}\}
and
{\bf S_{2}}=\{\{\neg q^{\sf p}\}\}
;

(iii)

{\bf T^{\prime}_{3}}={\bf T_{3}}\cup\{\{q^{\sf p}\}\}
and
{\bf S^{\prime}_{3}}={\bf S_{3}}\cup\{\{\neg p^{\sf p}\}\}
.

The Talmudic principle of Qal wa-ḥomer licenses the inference to the unconditional obligation ‘One ought not marry his daughter’
[
40
, p. 223]
. This corresponds to the following application of a suitable inference rule
qw
:

\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}

\varnothing\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\neg p

\delta_{\mathcal{P}_{1}}

\varnothing\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf P}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}q

\delta_{\mathcal{P}_{2}}

\varnothing\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf P}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-6.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S^{\prime}_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{7.7956pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T^{\prime}_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p

qw_{\mathcal{W}^{\sf o\star}}

\varnothing\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\neg q

with
{\bf T^{\prime}_{2}}={\bf T_{2}}\cup\{\{q^{\sf p}\}\}
,
{\bf S^{\prime}_{2}}={\bf S_{2}}\cup\{\{\neg p^{\sf p}\}\}
,
{\bf T}={\bf T_{1}}
,
{\bf S}=\{\{\neg\neg q^{\sf o}\}\}
and
\mathcal{W}^{\sf o\star}=\Big\{\dfrac{\top:\top}{\neg q}\Big\}
.

Control pairs can be further refined to address problematic scenarios involving the unrestricted weakening of obligations. A symmetric control pair is a structure of the form

\langle{\bf T},\langle{\bf S},{\bf S}^{\prime}\rangle\rangle

where
{\bf T}
collects sets of conditions,
{\bf S}
collects sets of constraints for formulas occurring in antecedent position, and
{\bf S}^{\prime}
collects sets of constraints for formulas occurring in succedent position. We can now adapt the notions of compatibility and soundness to this symmetric setting as follows.

Definition 4.1
.

Let
\langle{\bf T},{\mathfrak{S}}\rangle
be a symmetric control pair, with
{\mathfrak{S}}=\langle{\bf S},{\bf S^{\prime}}\rangle
.

(i)

\Delta
is compatible with
\langle{\bf T},{\mathfrak{S}}\rangle
exactly when
\overline{\overline{\mathsf{G4^{pn}}}}
refutes
A\vdash\bigvee\Delta
for every
A\in\bigcup{\bf S^{\prime}}
.

(ii)

\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.20834pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.20834pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.20834pt\raisebox{-5.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{S}}$}}\hskip-2.20834pt\hskip 2.20834pt}$}\hskip-2.20834pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
is sound if and only if
\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.51389pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.51389pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.51389pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S}$}}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip 2.51389pt}$}\hskip-2.51389pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
is sound and
\Delta
is compatible with
{\bf S^{\prime}}
.

(iii)

A
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-derivation
\pi
of
\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 3.14027pt\hskip-2.20834pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.20834pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.20834pt\raisebox{-5.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{S}}$}}\hskip-2.20834pt\hskip 2.20834pt}$}\hskip-2.20834pt\hskip-3.14027pt\raisebox{5.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T}$}}\hskip-3.14027pt\hskip 3.14027pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\Delta
is a proof if and only if the conclusion is sound and
\pi
is a proof when removing
{\bf S^{\prime}}
and its ancestors along
\pi
.

We end this section with the following applications of symmetric control pairs.

Example 4.14

(Ross’s paradox)
.

Let the extra-logical axiom and rules

\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}

\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p

\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}

\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.65967pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.65967pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.65967pt\raisebox{-5.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf S_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip 4.65967pt}$}\hskip-4.65967pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.80278pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\neg q

ax

r\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf F}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 2.04861pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{-6.25pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}$}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip-2.04861pt\raisebox{6.63889pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\varnothing$}}\hskip-2.04861pt\hskip 2.04861pt}\mkern 2.0mu}q

stand for the unconditional obligations ‘One ought to mail the letter’, ‘One ought not destroy the letter’ and the factual statement ‘If one burns the letter, she destroys it’, respectively – where
{\bf T_{1}}={\bf T_{2}}=\{\{\top^{\sf o}\}\}
,
{\bf S_{1}}=\{\{\neg p^{\sf o}\}\}
and
{\bf S_{2}}=\{\{\neg\neg q^{\sf o}\}\}
.

‘One mails the letter or one burns the letter’ can be inferred from ‘One mails the letter’ in classical logic, closure of obligations under classical consequence yields the unconditional obligation ‘One ought to post the letter or burn it’. This means that the obligation to mail the letter would entail an obligation that could be satisfied by burning it – an outcome that appears undesirable
[
39
]
.

To avoid this scenario and filter out the undesired instance of Right Weakening, we rewrite
\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}
replacing
{\bf S_{1}}
with
\mathfrak{S}_{1}=\langle{\bf S_{1}},\{\{p\vee q^{\sf o}\}\}\rangle
. As a result, the following derivation is a paraproof:

\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}

\varnothing\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.15976pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.15976pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.15976pt\raisebox{-5.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{S}_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.15976pt\hskip 4.15976pt}$}\hskip-4.15976pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p

LW^{\sf O}

\varnothing\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.15976pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.15976pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.15976pt\raisebox{-5.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{S}_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.15976pt\hskip 4.15976pt}$}\hskip-4.15976pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p,q

Example 4.15

(Good Samaritan paradox)
.

Let the following extra-logical rules

\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}^{\prime}

\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.15976pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.15976pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.15976pt\raisebox{-5.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{S}_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.15976pt\hskip 4.15976pt}$}\hskip-4.15976pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p

\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}^{\prime\prime}

\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.15976pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.15976pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.15976pt\raisebox{-5.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{S}_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.15976pt\hskip 4.15976pt}$}\hskip-4.15976pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}q

stand for the unconditional obligation ‘It ought to be that the Samaritan helps Jones whom
Smith has robbed’, and the extra-logical rule

\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{2}}

\Pi\mid\Gamma\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.15976pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.15976pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.15976pt\raisebox{-5.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{S}_{2}}$}}\hskip-4.15976pt\hskip 4.15976pt}$}\hskip-4.15976pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{2}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}\neg q

stand for the unconditional obligation ‘Smith ought not rob Jones’
[
37
]
– where
{\bf T_{1}}={\bf T_{2}}=\{\{\top^{\sf f}\}\}
,
{\mathfrak{S}_{1}}=\langle\{\{\neg(p\wedge q)^{\sf o}\}\},\{\{q^{\sf o}\}\}\rangle
and
{\mathfrak{S}_{2}}=\langle\{\{\neg\neg q^{\sf o}\}\},\{\{q^{\sf o}\}\}\rangle
. Consider the following derivation:

\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}^{\prime}

\varnothing\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.15976pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.15976pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.15976pt\raisebox{-5.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{S}_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.15976pt\hskip 4.15976pt}$}\hskip-4.15976pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p

\delta_{\mathcal{O}_{1}}^{\prime\prime}

\varnothing\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.15976pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.15976pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.15976pt\raisebox{-5.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{S}_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.15976pt\hskip 4.15976pt}$}\hskip-4.15976pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}q

\varnothing\mid\ \mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf O}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 5.28606pt\hskip-4.15976pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 4.15976pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip-4.15976pt\raisebox{-5.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\mathfrak{S}_{1}}$}}\hskip-4.15976pt\hskip 4.15976pt}$}\hskip-4.15976pt\hskip-5.28606pt\raisebox{6.78334pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\bf T_{1}}$}}\hskip-5.28606pt\hskip 5.28606pt}\mkern 2.0mu}p\wedge q

Such derivation is a proof, while the immediate leftmost subderivation is a paraproof. This reflects the fact that the obligation to help victims does not entail that the existence of victims is itself obligatory.

We conclude this section by observing that symmetric control pairs can be used to enforce the metaethical principle of ‘no vacuous
obligations’, according to which tautologies are never obligatory and contradictions are never prohibited
[
44
]
. Indeed, we could design
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculi without
{\sf O}
-labelled identity axioms, and in which every
{\sf O}
-labelled sequent includes, among its constraints, the singleton containing an arbitrary tautology for formulas in succedent position. Such
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculi would thereby capture deontic reasoning without allowing unconstrained Right Weakening
[
41
,
27
]
.

5.
Concluding remarks

In this paper, we introduced a uniform proof-theoretic framework for defaults, obligations, and permissions based on controlled sequent calculi. We defined deontic extensions, paralleling Łukasiewicz extensions for defaults and generalizing outfamilies in I/O logics. Controlled calculi were shown to admit contraction and non-analytic cut, to exhibit a weak form of analyticity, and to be strongly complete with respect to credulous consequence grounded on Łukasiewicz and deontic extensions. Moreover, they support weak versions of cumulative transitivity and cautious monotony, thus providing a unified and robust basis for deontic and nonmonotonic reasoning.

Several perspectives open up. First, control sets can be used to embed specific instances of paraconsistent reasoning into the classical base logic
[
28
]
. Furthermore, natural extensions of the framework seem capable of capturing default deontic reasoning on non-classical bases, particularly
{\bf FDE}
-based logics
[
31
]
.

It would be interesting to design
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculi in which extra-logical rules transmit arbitrary auxiliary formulas from the succedents of the premises to the succedent of the conclusion, thereby providing a uniform and modular treatment of
disjunctive
default and deontic reasoning
[
32
]
. With appropriate adjustments to the labelling of turnstiles and to the notion of soundness, it should also be possible to obtain
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculi for constrained I/O logics. Unlike the hybrid hypersequent calculi developed for I/O logics in
[
32
]
, these
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculi would enjoy strong adequacy.

Moreover, with respect to prohibitions understood as obligations to the contrary, certain contexts call for a more refined treatment, for instance, within Talmudic deontic logic
[
1
]
. It would therefore be of particular interest to integrate explicit prohibitions into the very definition of normative system, introducing a dedicated labelled turnstile
\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf PR}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{1.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt}\mkern 2.0mu}
to develop strongly complete
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculi. Additional extra-logical rules involving
\mathrel{{}_{{}_{{\sf PR}}}\mkern 2.0mu\rule[0.0pt]{0.4pt}{12.0pt}\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\hbox{$\rule[0.0pt]{10.76385pt}{0.4pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{-1.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt}$}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{1.0pt}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle$}}\hskip 0.0pt\hskip 0.0pt}\mkern 2.0mu}
could then capture the intricate interactions between prohibitions and other deontic notions.

Additionally, it would be worthwhile to examine whether
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
calculi can be employed to address prioritized default and normative reasoning
[
7
,
17
,
36
]
. Since prioritized
m
- and
d
-extensions lack the key property of semimonotonicity, defining a sound notion of
\mathsf{G4^{\delta}}
-proof capable of tracking prioritized credulous consequence may require a suitable notion of
hypothetical soundness
– i.e., soundness under the assumption that some formulas are not provable.
